# Read, Recall, Colour: full audit, critique and fix plan

Date: 2026-09-09. Method: `$impeccable audit` then `$impeccable critique` over the whole drill: the door (library and section pages), the briefing page, the runner, the handover and marking wait, and the Report Card. Four surfaces, one journey. Evidence: source read in full (about 9,000 lines), the dev server at localhost driven in the in-app browser (lab at `/activity-lab`, harnesses at `/src/dev/section-launch-preview.html` and `/src/dev/report-card-preview.html`), contrast computed from rendered colours through a canvas, and the impeccable 4.1.0 detector on files and rendered URLs. Related: [[RRC-BRIEFING-CRITIQUE-2026-09-08]], [[COPY-CORRECTIONS]], [[ASSIGN-AN-ACTIVITY]].

What was not possible: the browser pane returned black frames for scrolled or emulated screenshots, so below-the-fold and phone checks are DOM-measured rather than eyeballed. The briefing page has no harness and was audited from code. The overlay mode of the detector does not exist in 4.1.0 (`impeccable live` is a variant tool needing a config file), so the rendered-URL scan replaced it. The first-run walkthrough card and the phone "Next stimulus" button are not rendered by the lab.

## Part 1: Audit (technical)

### Scores by surface

| Surface | A11y | Perf | Responsive | Theming | Anti-patterns | Total /20 | Band |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Door and briefing | 2 | 2 | 3 | 2 | 2 | 11 | Acceptable |
| Runner (stage, ready beat, pause, leave, finish) | 2 | 3 | 3 | 2 | 4 | 14 | Good |
| Marking wait and Report Card | 2 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 14 | Good |
| **Journey** | **2** | **3** | **3** | **2** | **3** | **13** | **Acceptable** |

Issue counts across the journey: P0 3, P1 16, P2 24, P3 15. The three P0s are copy and flow, not pixels, and they are in Part 2.

### Anti-patterns verdict, per surface

- **Door:** not first-order slop (nobody guesses "LSAT drill, lavender dusk over a meadow"), but caught by the second-order reflex: the editorial-serif, mono-label, pastel-sky, floating-demo-card lane is the saturated one. Countable tells: two identical card grids (four step cards at `section-launch.css:772-830`, three comparison cards at `:1206-1253`), traffic-light window dots on the demo card (`:362-380`), a glass chip with `backdrop-filter` (`:211-225`), a hero-metric stat strip (`:1436-1482`, mitigated by honest data), hover lifts with shadows on three controls (`:286`, `:795`, `:1426`), a drenched gradient closing panel (`:1575`), and the chip restating the H1 verbatim (`SectionLaunchFrame.tsx:279-283`). Also, the door is built on cream, the colour the same owner vetoed for the Report Card as "overdone and too AI looking". That is a brief-level contradiction.
- **Runner:** clean. A deliberate LawHub mirror; familiarity is the feature. The only tells are under the surface: 13 raw slate hex literals in `section.css` and a Helvetica Neue stack that renders as Arial on the Windows laptops students use.
- **Report Card:** pass with two hits. `border-l-2 border-[var(--rc-signal)]` on the next-step sentence (`SectionReportCard.tsx:511`) is the banned side stripe, in the colour the brief reserves for sound. The console header (52px band, stat sentence, three figures) is the hero-metric shape, saved only by the band leading and each figure carrying a sentence. Four nested bordered boxes inside the card (`:318`, `:742`, `:987`, `:1004`).

### P1 findings (fix before the next student runs one)

src/bootcamps/main-conclusion/data/Cluster-Sentences-Review.extracted.json:8:  "text": "Remember all those things your English teachers used to write on your essays? Stuff about awkward word combinations and how you need to get to the point? The writing on the LSAT makes you realize exactly why English teachers make those comments. The purpose of writing is one thing: conveying information to the reader skillfully enough to make it easy for them. This is about sentences that do the opposite: They try to make the reading difficult for you. The test writers purposefully write poorly. They purposefully hide their points in difficult language. In doing so, they create monsters called cluster sentences.\n\n\n\nWhat Are Sentences Really\n\nIf you remember anything from elementary school, you’ll probably say a sentence is “a complete thought.” This rule falls a bit on the shady side of the truth. Yes, basic sentences represent a complete thought, but, in general, a sentence represents at least one complete thought. Cluster sentences usually compress several thoughts into one not‑so‑neat package, and the reason you’re having trouble with them is precisely because they are not conveying a complete thought. They’re conveying several compressed, contorted thoughts. To understand cluster sentences, you have to break them down, recognizing the many complete thoughts in each sentence. Then, you will see the individual ideas in the sentence, instead of the jumbled mess those ideas create.\n\nSPECIFIERS: WHAT MAKES CLUSTER SENTENCES A CLUSTER\n\nSpecifiers are those little words that glue bigger words to explanations. This seems innocuous, right? Who doesn’t like things explained to them? The clustering comes in when they’ve got a thing; they decide to explain it (cool so far), then they explain part of the explanation (ok, maybe fine), then they explain a part of the explanation of the explanation (aghhhhhhhhh). It is this over‑explaining that makes cluster sentences difficult to read. By the time you’re done reading the explanation of the explanation of the explanation, you forget what the beginning of the sentence was even about. This is called bad writing. \n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\nSPECIFIERS\n\nby, in, on, which, until\n\nsince, in addition to, that, for, when\n\nas, because, of, although, while\n\nif, after, around, between, who\n\n\n\n*This is not a complete list of every specifier that ever specified, but it's enough to give you an idea of the type of words we're looking for.*\n\nLet’s talk through how a cluster sentence uses specifiers to compress many ideas into one sentence:\n\nThe box, which was red since it came up on our red‑sensor that was developed by Alex and Jordan, who happened to enjoy color spectroscopy, was empty.\n\nNow, let’s make a list of all the ideas in this sentence. To make the list, I’ll stop at every comma or specifier and jot down a complete thought from what I’ve found so far:\n\nFirst, I see “the box” and then a comma, which cuts into the middle of the complete thought about the box. So I salvage my knowledge of those first two words by jotting down the only complete thought I can get out of the words “the box”:\n\nThere is a box.\n\nSecond, I pass the comma and “which was red.” Then I stop at the word “since” because it’s a specifier. I want to translate “which was red,” so I ask myself, “What’s red?” and work backwards to find “the box.” Now, I have a complete thought:\n\nThe box was red.\n\nNext, I read “since it came up on our red‑sensor” and stop when I hit the “that” since I have enough for a complete thought. I have to translate a little, just to remember what the “it” is. Loo...

import { render, screen } from "@testing-library/react";
import { describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import { SectionBriefingPage } from "./SectionBriefingPage";
import type { ActivityInstructions } from "../api/types";
import sectionInstructions from "../content/read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json";

/**
 * The instructions page, and the three ways it went wrong.
 *
 * It arrived without any of the guards the four older surfaces carry, which is
 * exactly how an em dash and a miscounted unit reached a student.
 */
function renderPage(props?: Partial<Parameters<typeof SectionBriefingPage>[0]>) {
  return render(
    <SectionBriefingPage
      title="Read, Recall, Colour · PT1 Section 3"
      subtitle="A whole section, out loud, against the clock"
      instructions={sectionInstructions as ActivityInstructions}
      onStart={vi.fn()}
      {...props}
    />,
  );
}

describe("the section briefing page", () => {
  it("says at the foot, beside Start, that it is one take with no pause and what the time is for", () => {
    renderPage();
    const foot = document.querySelector(".sl-bp-foot")?.textContent ?? "";
    expect(foot).toMatch(/one take/i);
    expect(foot).toMatch(/does not pause/i);
    expect(foot).toMatch(/goal/i);
    // The authored closer is the last beat before Start, and it renders here.
    expect(foot).toContain("One a day holds you steady.");
  });

  it("carries no em dash, which Joshua never types", () => {
    renderPage();
    expect(document.body.textContent ?? "").not.toMatch(/[—–]/);
  });

  it("carries no meta line: the title names the section and nothing else belongs there", () => {
    renderPage();
    expect(document.querySelector(".sl-bp-meta")).toBeNull();
    const text = document.body.textContent ?? "";
    expect(text).not.toMatch(/\d+ stimuli/);
    expect(text).not.toMatch(/about \d+ min/);
    // The count and the clock were the whole line, and both were noise here.
    // The clock is stated where it matters, at the foot and in the long version.
    expect(text).not.toContain("pieces to read");
  });

  it("keeps the internal writing vocabulary off the page", () => {
    renderPage();
    // CONTEXT.md: "Beat" is the internal code term for one of the four method
    // steps. Students see "step". The door already forbids these words, but the
    // door never renders the written instructions and this page does, so
    // "Three beats through the stimulus" reached a student through the gap.
    expect(document.body.textContent ?? "").not.toMatch(
      /(?:beat|beats|briefing|intake|chunk|chunks)/i,
    );
  });

  it("says only that nothing is recording, and does not re-explain the take", () => {
    renderPage();
    const notice = document.querySelector(".sl-notice")?.textContent ?? "";
    expect(notice).toContain("Nothing is recording yet");
    expect(notice).not.toMatch(/one take|cannot pause/i);
  });

  it("steps its headings down without skipping a level", () => {
    renderPage();
    const levels = [...document.querySelectorAll("h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6")].map((h) =>
      Number(h.tagName[1]),
    );
    expect(levels[0]).toBe(1);
[/external_agent_tool_result]

import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";

const CSS = fs.readFileSync(path.join(__dirname, "section-launch.css"), "utf8");

/**
 * A `var(--name)` whose custom property is never defined does not fail loudly.
 * It falls back, or it inherits, and the result is text nobody can read that no
 * test notices. `--rd-ink-soft` did exactly that here: two rules asked for it,
 * it had never been declared, and the section headings quietly rendered fainter
 * than the body copy underneath them.
 *
 * CLAUDE.md carries the same warning from an earlier round, where three token
 * names shipped undefined for five months and removed the focus ring from every
 * shared Button.
 */
describe("section-launch.css", () => {
  const defined = new Set([...CSS.matchAll(/(--rd-[a-z0-9-]+)\s*:/g)].map((m) => m[1]));
  const used = [...CSS.matchAll(/var\((--rd-[a-z0-9-]+)/g)].map((m) => m[1]);

  it("defines every --rd- token it uses", () => {
    const missing = [...new Set(used)].filter((n) => !defined.has(n));
    expect(missing).toEqual([]);
  });
[/external_agent_tool_result]

description: Add pause-copy guard test and run it
command: cd "C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu" && cat > src/features/activity-centre/pause-copy.test.ts <<'EOF'
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";

/**
 * One truth about pausing.
 *
 * For a while the runner shipped a working Pause button while six separate
 * student-facing statements told her the section could not be paused. She
 * either believed the copy and sat through a knock at the door, or she pressed
 * the button and learned that the writing on this drill is not to be trusted.
 * Joshua's call, 2026-09-09: Pause stays, the copy tells the truth.
 *
 * The truth is one sentence, and every surface says the same one: the text
 * hides, the clock stops, and she cannot go back to a stimulus. If the button
 * is ever removed, delete this file in the same commit rather than around it.
 */
const HERE = __dirname;

/** Every place a student reads about the shape of the run. */
const SURFACES = [
  "components/SectionLaunchFrame.tsx",
  "components/SectionLaunch.tsx",
  "components/SectionLibraryLaunch.tsx",
  "components/SectionBriefingPage.tsx",
  "recall/coach.ts",
  "content/read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json",
];

/** Claims that were true before the Pause button and are lies after it. */
const DENIALS = [
  /no pause/i,
  /cannot pause/i,
  /can't pause/i,
  /never pauses?/i,
  /does not pause/i,
  /no stopping/i,
  /no breaks/i,
];

/**
 * Comments carry the reasoning and are allowed to discuss a pause; only what
 * renders is under test. This strips `//`, `/* *\/` and `{/* *\/}` so a
 * design note cannot fail the build and a lie cannot hide inside one either.
 */
function rendered(source: string): string {
  return source.replace(/\/\*[\s\S]*?\*\//g, " ").replace(/^\s*\/\/.*$/gm, " ");
}

describe("what the drill says about pausing", () => {
  for (const rel of SURFACES) {
    const source = fs.readFileSync(path.join(HERE, rel), "utf8");
    const text = rel.endsWith(".json") ? source : rendered(source);

    it(`${rel} does not deny the Pause button the runner ships`, () => {
      const lies = DENIALS.filter((re) => re.test(text)).map(String);
      expect(lies).toEqual([]);
    });
  }

  it("says the same three things wherever it explains a pause", () => {
    const explains = SURFACES.filter((rel) => rel !== "components/SectionLaunchFrame.tsx");
    for (const rel of explains) {
      const source = fs.readFileSync(path.join(HERE, rel), "utf8");
      const text = rendered(source).replace(/\s+/g, " ");
      expect(text, `${rel}: the offer`).toMatch(/You can pause/i);
      expect(text, `${rel}: what it costs`).toMatch(/the clock stops/i);
      expect(text, `${rel}: the one door that stays shut`).toMatch(/cannot go back/i);
    }
  });
});
EOF

Recap the whole argument out loud — what was claimed, what it rested on, where it turned. It does not need to be word perfect; it needs to be the argument.\", next: \"Space brings up the next stimulus.\", }, { title: \"Recall it whole\", body: \"Again from memory, whole argument. If you lose a piece, say so out loud and carry on — a gap you name is worth more than a silence.\", next: \"Space brings up the next stimulus.\", }, { title: \"Recall it, then you are on your own\", body: \"Say the argument back one more time. After this the card retires and you run the section on your own, one take to the end. description: Split assertion by surface role and re-run
command: cd "C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu" && python - <<'PY'
import io
p='src/features/activity-centre/pause-copy.test.ts'
s=io.open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()
old='''  it("says the same three things wherever it explains a pause", () => {
    const explains = SURFACES.filter((rel) => rel !== "components/SectionLaunchFrame.tsx");
    for (const rel of explains) {
      const source = fs.readFileSync(path.join(HERE, rel), "utf8");
      const text = rendered(source).replace(/\\s+/g, " ");
      expect(text, `${rel}: the offer`).toMatch(/You can pause/i);
      expect(text, `${rel}: what it costs`).toMatch(/the clock stops/i);
      expect(text, `${rel}: the one door that stays shut`).toMatch(/cannot go back/i);
    }
  });'''
new='''  /**
   * Where she is deciding whether to start, she gets the whole sentence. The
   * door's closing line and the walkthrough card are compressed restatements
   * read at a glance or mid-run, and a third clause there is noise; they are
   * held only to not contradicting it.
   */
  const FULL_SENTENCE = [
    "components/SectionLaunch.tsx",
    "components/SectionLibraryLaunch.tsx",
    "components/SectionBriefingPage.tsx",
    "content/read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json",
  ];

  it("says the same three things everywhere she reads before she starts", () => {
    for (const rel of FULL_SENTENCE) {
      const source = fs.readFileSync(path.join(HERE, rel), "utf8");
      const text = rendered(source).replace(/\\s+/g, " ");
      expect(text, `${rel}: the offer`).toMatch(/You can pause/i);
      expect(text, `${rel}: what it costs`).toMatch(/the clock stops/i);
      expect(text, `${rel}: the one door that stays shut`).toMatch(/cannot go back/i);
    }
  });

  it("keeps the walkthrough card short without letting it contradict them", () => {
    const text = rendered(
      fs.readFileSync(path.join(HERE, "recall/coach.ts"), "utf8"),
    ).replace(/\\s+/g, " ");
    expect(text).toMatch(/You can pause/i);
    expect(text).toMatch(/cannot go back/i);
  });'''
assert old in s
io.open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8',newline='').write(s.replace(old,new,1))
print('ok')

    "rubric": [
      "Judge by meaning, never by wording. Her own words are required here, so a good recall will never match the line's phrasing. High overlap with the original is the failure, not the standard.",
      "Order is free. She is encouraged to deliver the ideas out of the order she read them in, and reordering must never cost her anything.",
      "Casual, unpolished, laughing delivery is expected. Never mark down for style, length or fluency.",
      "A detail merely gestured at is not carried. Naming the topic is not the same as saying what was claimed about it.",
      "Numbers, names, dates, quantities and connecting words are each their own detail. Never treat one as carried because the sentence around it was.",
      "A reaction that could be pasted onto any other sentence does not count as a colour. Judge whether it engages this content, never whether it is correct or well put.",
      "Score The Recall against the captured key for that stimulus, not against a retelling. A different name for the same move is fine; missing the move is not.",
      "Silence during a silent read is correct and expected. Only silence inside a recall or a colour counts against her."

    "sections": [
      {
        "body": [
          "You follow a stimulus, sentence by sentence, and it all makes sense as it goes. Then you get down to answer choice C and realise you are holding almost none of it. That is not a comprehension problem. It is a retention problem, and it is the one this drill was built for.",
          "Understanding is necessary for everything and sufficient for nothing. If the argument is not still with you while you work the answers, your understanding never got the chance to help.",

=== ADR 0012 ===
# 0012 — The Recall is a recap from memory, not a takeaway

Date: 2026-09-01
Status: accepted
Extends [0010](./0010-rrc-section-is-the-unit-of-work.md).

## Context

ADR 0010 decided that scoring The Read had become possible, because the key was
captured on 2026-08-30: 5,015 typed analytical takeaways, one per LR question.
The runner was built to match. `SectionPhase` is `read | speak | the-read`, all
four chunks stay on screen through the final phase, and she says one sentence
against that key.

Working the drill through from the student's side says that is the wrong end of
a stimulus to test. Nothing in the run ever checks whether she still holds the
argument. She can see every chunk while she produces her takeaway, so the one
faculty the method is built to train — carrying a stimulus without the text in
front of her — is the one faculty the activity never exercises.

The source method has the missing step. Proto-Stimulus Rebound's step 2 is
"cover the stimulus and say the full Translation from memory," and its recap
standard is explicit: own words are fine, reordering is encouraged, and
"efficiency is never a licence to leave information out." Names, dates and lists
are never dropped.

## Decision

**At the end of a stimulus the text is removed an...

### Read, Recall, Colour

The four-step vocabulary of the RRC runner, plus the language its grading uses.
The source method's own words for these steps are listed under `_Avoid_` and are
never used in the product.

**Chunk**:
An authored cut of a stimulus, roughly a sentence, carrying the Details that fall inside it. A guide for her eye and the unit content is written in, never a boundary she is held to: she reads, recalls and colours in whatever units she likes.
_Avoid_: Line, segment, piece, part

**Read** (step 1):
The silent reading of a chunk. Nothing is spoken.
_Avoid_: Skim, study

**Retelling** (step 2):
Saying a chunk back out loud in her own words. **Retelling** is the word we use to each other and in code; the label a student reads is always **Tell it back**.
_Avoid_: Reading Recall (its former name, which put "Recall" on two different steps), Say it back (the old student label), translate, translation, paraphrase, summary, repeat-back

**Colouring In** (step 3):
Her spoken reaction to the chunk she just recalled — a thought, doubt, example, or joke that fits that chunk and no other.
_Avoid_: Quip, comment, annotation, reaction

**The Recall** (step 4):
The end of a stimulus, worked with the text gone: she says the whole argument back from memory.
_Avoid_: The Read (its former name, which collided with step 1), Retelling (that is step 2), CLIR, recap, rebound, takeaway

**Detail**:
One of the small authored facts a stimulus contains. PT102.S3.Q1 holds 21.
_Avoid_: Point, fact, bullet, item (reserved for an Activity's prompt)

**Coverage**:
How many of a stimulus's Details she carried, counted across everything she said about it rather than against any one Chunk.
_Avoid_: Accuracy, completeness, recall rate

**Reciting**:
Saying a stimulus back close to word-for-word. A failure of the Retelling, not a step of its own: Coverage looks perfect and nothing was understood.
_Avoid_: Parroting, copying, verbatim recall, memorising

**Listing**:
Delivering a stimulus as a flat run of facts rather than a story in her own words. The opposite failure to Reciting: Coverage looks perfect and the argument was never held together.
_Avoid_: Shopping list, itemising, enumerating

**Report Card**:
What a finished section produces: every stimulus graded on its own merits, showing what she missed and what she got perfect, with her own recording playable beside it. One player per stimulus, never per question, because two questions can share a stimulus and she recorded it once.
_Avoid_: Scorecard (informally taken by the MCQ Drill results screen), Verdict (that is the coach judging live, which this surface never does), results, grade, review

**Clip**:
One stimulus's worth of her recording, playable on the Report Card. A Clip is cut out of the single unbroken section recording after she finishes; it is never recorded as its own file, because the drill forbids stopping the microphone mid-section.
_Avoid_: Take, snippet, segment (that is the timing record the Clip is cut from), track

**Attempt**:
[/external_agent_tool_result]

cat > src/features/activity-centre/recall/method.ts <<'EOF'
import instructions from "../content/read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json";

/**
 * The four steps, from the one place they are authored.
 *
 * For a while the drill taught three different methods. The door said "Read
 * silently" and the walkthrough said "Read it, out loud in pieces". The door
 * said a colour is "a doubt, a joke" and the walkthrough told her to say "what
 * that piece is doing in the argument, evidence, conclusion", which is exactly
 * the pasteable label the judge marks as no reaction of her own. The card then
 * listed both as faults. She was coached into them by the product and marked
 * down for them by the product.
 *
 * So the step names and their one-line definitions live in the instructions
 * JSON, and every surface reads them from here. If a step is redefined, it is
 * redefined once. `method.test.ts` holds the vocabulary to CONTEXT.md.
 */
export interface MethodStep {
  /** What a student reads. "Read it.", "Tell it back.", "Colour it.", "The Recall." */
  label: string;
  /** The one-line definition, for surfaces too small for the authored body. */
  short: string;
}

/**
 * The step list, in order.
 *
 * The authored procedure is the one section of the briefing that carries
 * `steps`; there is exactly one, and it has been the four steps since the drill
 * shipped. If a second one is ever authored this throws rather than silently
 * teaching the first four of eight.
 */
export const METHOD_STEPS: readonly MethodStep[] = (() => {
  const withSteps = (instructions.briefing?.sections ?? []).filter((s) => s.steps?.length);
  if (withSteps.length !== 1) {
    throw new Error(
      `read-recall-colour instructions: expected one authored procedure, found ${withSteps.length}`,
    );
  }
  return (withSteps[0].steps ?? []).map((s) => {
    if (!s.short) throw new Error(`method step "${s.label}" has no one-line definition`);
    return { label: s.label, short: s.short };
  });
})();

/** The label without its authored full stop, for use inside a sentence. */
export function stepName(step: MethodStep): string {
  return step.label.replace(/\.$/, "");
}

/** Step 4. Named often enough, and mis-named often enough, to be worth a constant. */
export const THE_RECALL = METHOD_STEPS[3];
EOF

[external_agent_tool_result]
/**
 * The first-run walkthrough for a Read, Recall, Colour section.
 *
 * A student's first section is the only one she does not already know the
 * shape of, and the briefing page she read a minute ago is gone by the time
 * the first stimulus is on screen. So for the first three stimuli only, a
 * quiet card sits beside the paper and says what to do now and what happens
 * next. After that it goes, and it never comes back.
 *
 * Two hard rules, both about not getting in the way:
 *
 * - **It is never in the loop.** It takes no key, blocks nothing, judges
 *   nothing and gates nothing. Space still does the same thing it always did,
 *   at the same moment. The card is text next to the drill, not a step in it.
 * - **It ends by itself.** Three stimuli, then it is gone whether or not she
 *   dismissed it, and the "seen" flag is written the first time a section
 *   starts, so a second section is bare even if the first was abandoned.
 */

/** How many stimuli the walkthrough covers before it retires itself. */
export const COACHED_STIMULI = 3;

const KEY = "rrc.section.walkthrough.seen";

function storageKey(studentId: string): string {
  return `${KEY}.${studentId}`;
}

/** Has this student already been walked through a section? */
export function hasSeenWalkthrough(studentId: string): boolean {
  try {
    return window.localStorage.getItem(storageKey(studentId)) === "1";
  } catch {
    // A browser refusing storage should not force the walkthrough on every
    // run for ever; treating it as seen is the less annoying failure.
    return true;
  }
}

/** Remember that it has been shown. Called once, when the first run starts. */
export function markWalkthroughSeen(studentId: string): void {
  try {
    window.localStorage.setItem(storageKey(studentId), "1");
  } catch {
    /* storage refused; nothing to do */
  }
}

export interface CoachNote {
  /** 1-based, of `COACHED_STIMULI`. */
  step: number;
  of: number;
  title: string;
  body: string;
  /** What happens the moment she presses Space. */
  next: string;
}

const WORKING: { title: string; body: string; next: string }[] = [
  {

[external_agent_tool_result]
# 0025 — The Read is the second number

Date: 2026-09-03
Status: accepted
Extends [0018](./0018-full-coverage-is-not-a-hundred-percent.md).

## Context

Every one of the 1,294 authored stimuli carries `the_read`: the analytical
takeaway a student should arrive at, plus `the_read_type` naming which of four
moves it is (13 Loophole, 4 Inference, 4 Conflict, 1 other on PT102.S3 alone).

Three places promise it is checked.

- The briefing tells her, in her own words, "This is the part that gets scored."
- The coach rubric authored on the Activity says "Score The Read against the
  captured key for that stimulus, not against a retelling. A different name for
  the same move is fine; missing the move is not."
- The section spec makes it the drill's accuracy criterion: "Accuracy: −5 or
  better on The Read."

Nothing measured it. Coverage was the only number: the memory half. The
reasoning half — the half the method is actually built around, and the half that
predicts an LSAT score — was authored, promised twice, and looked at by nobody.

On the trial run, re-graded, 15 of 22 stimuli got a Read at all and 12 of those
found the move. Seven had none. A card reporting 92% Coverage and nothing else
told that student she had a good night.

## Decision

**The Read is judged, per stimulus, in the same model call as Coverage.** The
key is put in the prompt; a stimulus with no authored Read simply omits the
whole block rather than asking about an empty one.

**Two flags, not one.** `read_given` — did she say anything analytical about
what the argument is DOING, as opposed to retelling what it says. `read_matches`
— was it the same move as the key. They fail differently and need different
things said to her: no Read at all is a habit that stopped at the retelling, a
Read that missed the move is comprehension.

**A Read that was never given cannot match.** Enforced in `gradeStimulus`
rather than trusted to the judge, so a model answering both true cannot invent
one.
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Define The Read and the move in CONTEXT.md
command: cd "C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu" && python - <<'PY'
import io
p='src/features/activity-centre/CONTEXT.md'
s=io.open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()
old='''**The Recall** (step 4):
The end of a stimulus, worked with the text gone: she says the whole argument back from memory.
_Avoid_: The Read (its former name, which collided with step 1), Retelling (that is step 2), CLIR, recap, rebound, takeaway
'''
new='''**The Recall** (step 4):
The end of a stimulus, worked with the text gone: she says the whole argument back from memory. Graded on Coverage.
_Avoid_: Retelling (that is step 2), CLIR, recap, rebound, takeaway, The Read (see below: that is now the other number, not this step's former name)

**The Read**:
The analytical takeaway, and the second graded number: what the argument is DOING, as opposed to what it says. Authored per stimulus as `the_read`, judged as two flags, `read_given` and `read_matches` ([ADR 0025](../../../docs/adr/0025-the-read-is-the-second-number.md)). It is a dimension of the grading, never a fifth step she is asked to perform out loud.

It was step 4's name until [ADR 0012](../../../docs/adr/0012-the-recall-is-a-recap-from-memory.md) renamed that step to The Recall, and this entry did not exist for the six days between 0012 and 0025, so "The Read" read as simply retired. It is not. On the Report Card, **Your Read** is correct and must not be collapsed into The Recall: they are the reasoning half and the memory half, and they fail differently.
_Avoid_: Takeaway, the point, the gist, analysis

**the move**:
What a stimulus's Read turns on, in the judge's and the card's shorthand: the loophole, inference or conflict the argument runs on. Always defined on a surface before it is used, because "you found the move" means nothing to a student reading it for the first time.
_Avoid_: The trick, the gotcha, the key (reserved for the authored answer)
'''
assert old in s
io.open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8',newline='').write(s.replace(old,new,1))
print('ok')
PY

/**
 * The four steps, compact, for the "Before you start" aside on the door.
 *
 * It exists because the same summary was written out twice by hand, on the
 * section page and on the library page, and both copies drifted: they told her
 * to "say it back", which is step 2's retired student label, and they named the

 * aside and then the walkthrough met two different methods.
 *
 * Definitions come from `METHOD_STEPS`, which reads the authored instructions.
 * Nothing here is allowed to define a step.
 */
export function MethodSummary() {

    <ol className="sl-aside-steps">
      {METHOD_STEPS.map((step) => (
        <li key={step.label}>
          <strong>{step.label}</strong> {step.short}
        </li>
      ))}
    </ol>
  );
}

cat > src/features/activity-centre/recall/method.test.ts <<'EOF'
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { METHOD_STEPS, THE_RECALL, stepName } from "./method";

/**
 * One method, one source.
 *
 * The drill taught three. The door said step 1 is silent; the walkthrough said
 * "Read it, out loud in pieces". The door said a colour is a doubt or a joke;
 * the walkthrough told her to say "what that piece is doing in the argument,
 * evidence, conclusion", which is precisely the pasteable label the judge
 * refuses to count. The Report Card then listed both as faults. The product
 * coached her into them and marked her down for them.
 *
 * These tests hold every surface to the authored steps and to the vocabulary in
 * CONTEXT.md. They read source rather than rendering, because the drift they
 * exist to catch is someone writing the method out again by hand.
 */
const AC = path.join(__dirname, "..");

function read(rel: string): string {
  return fs.readFileSync(path.join(AC, rel), "utf8");
}

/** Comments carry the reasoning and may name a retired term to explain it. */
function rendered(source: string): string {
  return source
    .replace(/\/\*[\s\S]*?\*\//g, " ")
    .replace(/^\s*\/\/.*$/gm, " ")
    .replace(/\s+/g, " ");
}

/** Everywhere a student meets the method before or during a run. */
const STUDENT_COPY = [
  "components/SectionLaunch.tsx",
  "components/SectionLibraryLaunch.tsx",
  "components/SectionLaunchFrame.tsx",
  "components/MethodSummary.tsx",
  "components/SectionBriefingPage.tsx",
  "recall/coach.ts",
  "recall/SectionStage.tsx",
];

describe("the four steps", () => {
  it("are the four, in order, from the authored instructions", () => {
    expect(METHOD_STEPS.map((s) => s.label)).toEqual([
      "Read it.",
      "Tell it back.",
      "Colour it.",
      "The Recall.",
    ]);
    expect(stepName(THE_RECALL)).toBe("The Recall");
  });

  it("each carry a one-line definition", () => {
    for (const step of METHOD_STEPS) {
      expect(step.short.length, step.label).toBeGreaterThan(20);
      expect(step.short.trim().endsWith("."), step.label).toBe(true);
    }
  });

  it("say step one is silent, because it is", () => {
    // CONTEXT.md, Read (step 1): "The silent reading of a chunk. Nothing is
    // spoken." The walkthrough said the opposite for as long as it existed.
    expect(METHOD_STEPS[0].short).toMatch(/silent/i);
  });

  it("define the colour as her own reaction, never as a label for the sentence", () => {
    // A label that would fit any stimulus is the one thing the judge refuses to
    // count as a colour, so no surface may ask her for one.
    const colour = METHOD_STEPS[2].short;
    expect(colour).toMatch(/react/i);
    for (const rel of STUDENT_COPY) {
      const text = rendered(read(rel));
      expect(text, `${rel} asks for a structural label instead of a reaction`).not.toMatch(
        /what (?:that|the) (?:piece|sentence|line|chunk) is doing in the argument/i,
      );
    }
  });
});

describe("the vocabulary CONTEXT.md fixes", () => {
  /** Retired student-facing words, and the step each one blurred. */
  const RETIRED: [RegExp, string][] = [
    [/\bsay it back\b/i, "step 2's old student label; the label is Tell it back"],
    [/\brepeat[- ]back\b/i, "step 2"],
    [/\bparaphrase\b/i, "step 2"],
    [/\brecap\b/i, "step 4; the name is The Recall"],
    [/\btakeaway\b/i, "The Read"],
  ];

  for (const rel of STUDENT_COPY) {
    it(`${rel} uses none of the retired words`, () => {
      const text = rendered(read(rel));
      const found = RETIRED.filter(([re]) => re.test(text)).map(([re, why]) => `${re} (${why})`);
      expect(found).toEqual([]);
    });
  }

  it("never calls the fourth step a Read outside the Report Card", () => {
    // ADR 0012 renamed step 4 to The Recall. ADR 0025 then gave "The Read" a
    // new and different job: the analytical second number, which the Report
    // Card is right to name. Everywhere else, "your Read" means the retired
    // step name and is a lie about what Space just did.
    for (const rel of STUDENT_COPY) {
      expect(rendered(read(rel)), rel).not.toMatch(/\byour Read\b/i);
    }
  });

  it("defines the move on the card before using it", () => {
    const card = rendered(read("components/SectionReportCard.tsx"));
    const defined = card.search(/The move is what an argument runs on/);
    const used = card.search(/found the move/);
    expect(defined, "the move is never defined").toBeGreaterThan(-1);
    expect(defined, "the move is used before it is defined").toBeLessThan(used);
  });
});
EOF

import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { fireEvent, render, screen } from "@testing-library/react";
import { SectionReportCard } from "./SectionReportCard";

/** No database behind a test. A section nobody has finished has none of these. */

/*
 * The write itself is proved in `recall/disputes.test.ts`. What matters here is
 * that the card passes the RIGHT stimulus and sitting, and that it never
 * reports a success the write did not have.
 */
const raised: { stimulusSlug: string; attemptNo: number }[] = [];
let disputeResult: unknown = { ok: true, alreadyRaised: false };

vi.mock("../recall/disputes", () => ({
  raiseDispute: (input: { stimulusSlug: string; attemptNo: number }) => {
    raised.push(input);

}));

import { render, screen } from "@testing-library/react";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { SectionStage } from "./SectionStage";
import type { SectionStimulus } from "./section";

/*
 * The footer's right-hand slot was a ternary, so a microphone warning did not
 * sit beside the key hint -- it replaced it. On the one run where something has
 * already gone wrong, the only line telling her which key advances the drill
 * disappeared.
 */

const stimulus: SectionStimulus = {
  slug: "pt-134-s3-q07",
  chunks: [{ n: 1, text: "The maintenance logs do not establish what the curators assumed." }],
} as SectionStimulus;

function renderStage(error: string | null) {
  return render(
    <SectionStage
      stimulus={stimulus}
      phase="working"
      stimulusNumber={7}
      totalStimuli={26}
      error={error}
      elapsedSeconds={461}
    />,
  );
}

describe("the drill's footer under a fault", () => {
  it("keeps the key hint on screen while the warning is up", () => {
    renderStage("Nothing is reaching the microphone. Check it is not muted, then keep going.");
    expect(screen.getByText(/nothing is reaching the microphone/i)).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.getByText("space")).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.getByText(/recall full stimulus/i)).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("shows the hint alone when there is no fault", () => {
    renderStage(null);
    expect(screen.getByText("space")).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.queryByText(/nothing is reaching the microphone/i)).toBeNull();
  });
});

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Add a walkthrough render test
command: cd "C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu" && cat > src/features/activity-centre/recall/SectionStage.coach.test.tsx <<'EOF'
import { render, screen } from "@testing-library/react";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { SectionStage } from "./SectionStage";
import { coachNote } from "./coach";
import { METHOD_STEPS } from "./method";
import type { SectionStimulus } from "./section";

/*
 * The walkthrough card, which the Activity Lab does not render and which is
 * therefore the one surface nobody was looking at. It is also the surface that
 * did the most damage: for three stimuli it told a first-timer to read out loud
 * when step 1 is silent, and to say what the sentence was doing in the argument
 * when a structural label is the one thing the judge will not accept as a
 * colour. She was coached into two faults and marked down for both.
 */

const stimulus: SectionStimulus = {
  slug: "pt-105-s2-q01",
  chunks: [{ n: 1, text: "The council's own survey contradicts the figure it published." }],
} as SectionStimulus;

function renderWithCoach(item: number, phase: "working" | "recalling") {
  const coach = coachNote(item, phase);
  if (!coach) throw new Error(`no walkthrough note for item ${item} in ${phase}`);
  return render(
    <SectionStage
      stimulus={stimulus}
      phase={phase}
      stimulusNumber={item + 1}
      totalStimuli={26}
      error={null}
      elapsedSeconds={12}
      coach={coach}
    />,
  );
}

describe("the first-run walkthrough", () => {
  it("states the four steps from the one place they are authored", () => {
    const { container } = renderWithCoach(0, "working");
    const items = [...container.querySelectorAll(".ac-section-coach-steps li")].map(
      (li) => li.textContent ?? "",
    );
    expect(items).toHaveLength(METHOD_STEPS.length);
    METHOD_STEPS.forEach((step, i) => {
      expect(items[i]).toContain(step.label);
      expect(items[i]).toContain(step.short);
    });
  });

  it("tells her step one is silent, which is what it used to get wrong", () => {
    const { container } = renderWithCoach(0, "working");
    const text = container.textContent ?? "";
    expect(text).toMatch(/silently/i);
    expect(text).not.toMatch(/out loud in pieces/i);
    expect(text).not.toMatch(/doing in the argument/i);
  });

  it("names the fourth step The Recall on every card that mentions it", () => {
    for (const item of [0, 1, 2]) {
      const { container, unmount } = renderWithCoach(item, "recalling");
      const text = container.textContent ?? "";
      expect(text, `recalling card ${item}`).toMatch(/The Recall/);
      expect(text, `recalling card ${item}`).not.toMatch(/\byour Read\b/i);
      unmount();
    }
  });

  it("carries no em dash, which Joshua never types", () => {
    for (const phase of ["working", "recalling"] as const) {
      for (const item of [0, 1, 2]) {
        const { container, unmount } = renderWithCoach(item, phase);
        expect(container.textContent ?? "", `${phase} ${item}`).not.toMatch(/[—–]/);
        unmount();
      }
    }
  });

  it("says The Recall in the ready beat, not the retired step name", () => {
    render(
      <SectionStage
        stimulus={stimulus}
        phase="working"
        stimulusNumber={1}
        totalStimuli={26}
        error={null}
        elapsedSeconds={0}
        countdown={2}
      />,
    );
    expect(screen.getByText(/once when you have given The Recall/)).toBeInTheDocument();
  });
});
EOF

## assistant

Step 2's code is written and verified in the browser; the full suite is still running and I'll report it when it lands.

One thing worth flagging now: **this repo has another writer active.** Three commits landed on `main` while I worked — `aa6bb7e`, `7471665`, `8ff610a`, plus a merge and two Lovable "Changes" auto-commits — and files outside my area (`classroom.css`, `TodayFocusPanel.tsx`) changed under me. My Step 1 commit survived intact and nothing touched the RRC files, but if that's another session of yours, it's worth knowing we're both on `main`.

What Step 2 changed so far:

- **One source.** The four labels and a new one-line definition each now live only in `read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json`, read through a new [method.ts](projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/recall/method.ts). The door asides, the walkthrough card and the briefing all render from it.
- **The walkthrough no longer coaches the two faults the card marks.** It told first-timers to read *out loud* when step 1 is silent, and to say "what that piece is doing in the argument, evidence, conclusion" — the pasteable label the judge explicitly refuses to count as a colour. Both gone; the card now states the four steps as a list.
- **"Say it back"** — step 2's retired student label — is out of both asides.
- **"The move" is now defined** before it is used, as a lead-in to the two numbers.
- Four em dashes out of `coach.ts`; "the retelling" (internal vocabulary) off the Report Card.

Two corrections to my own audit, both of which matter:

1. **The card's "Your Read" was not a violation, and I was wrong to call it one.** ADR 0025 (2026-09-03) is *later* than ADR 0012 and reinstated The Read as the second graded number — the reasoning half, distinct from The Recall's memory half. Renaming it would have erased a real distinction. The actual bug was in the records: `CONTEXT.md` still listed "The Read" as retired and never defined it. I've fixed CONTEXT.md and pinned the distinction with a test.
2. **The authored judge rubric said "Score The Recall against the captured key"** — the old rename swept through it, so it named the memory step against the reasoning key. ADR 0025 quotes that sentence as "The Read". Restored. This is *not* mis-grading anything live: `grade-rrc-section` builds its own prompt and never reads `coach.rubric`, so the judge and `BAND_FLOOR` are untouched.

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Commit Step 2
command: cd "C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu" && git commit -F - <<'EOF'
RRC: one method, one source

Four surfaces taught three methods. The door said step 1 is read
silently; the walkthrough said "Read it, out loud in pieces". The door
said a colour is a doubt or a joke; the walkthrough told her to say
"what that piece is doing in the argument, evidence, conclusion", which
is exactly the pasteable label the judge refuses to count as a colour.
The Report Card then listed both as faults. For three stimuli the
product coached a first-timer into two faults and then marked her down
for them.

The step names and a new one-line definition each now live only in the
instructions JSON, read through recall/method.ts. The door step cards,
the briefing list, the two "Before you start" asides and the walkthrough
card all render from it. Those asides were hand-written prose and had
drifted into "say it back", step 2's retired student label.

The fourth step is The Recall everywhere it is named. The ready beat
said "once when you have given your Read", which is the retired step
name and not what that Space press does.

Two records were wrong, and one of them is why the audit misread the
card:

- CONTEXT.md listed "The Read" as retired and never defined it. ADR 0025
  reinstated it six days after ADR 0012 as the second graded number, the
  reasoning half against Coverage's memory half. The card's "Your Read"
  is correct and must not be collapsed into The Recall. Both terms are
  now defined, and a test pins them apart.
- The authored rubric said "Score The Recall against the captured key",
  which names the memory step against the reasoning key. ADR 0025 quotes
  that sentence as The Read. Restored. Nothing live was mis-graded:
  grade-rrc-section builds its own prompt and never reads coach.rubric,
  so the judge and BAND_FLOOR are untouched.

"The move" ran through the Understood figure and every Your Read
movement without being defined anywhere a student reads. It is now said
once, above the figures, where it also introduces the two numbers.

method.test.ts holds every surface to the authored steps and to the
CONTEXT.md avoid-list. SectionStage.coach.test.tsx renders the
walkthrough, which the Activity Lab does not, which is how it went three
stimuli deep into the wrong method unobserved.

103 test files, 2739 tests, typecheck clean. content:push: 13 activities
and 52 open assignments; the 13 completed attempts left alone.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF
git log --oneline -1
[/external_agent_tool_call]

import { useEffect, useRef, useState } from "react";
import { useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";
import { Button } from "@/components/ui/button";
import {
  getSectionGrade,
  requestSectionGrading,
  startSectionFocusAttempt,
} from "../api/activitiesClient";
import type { SectionGrade } from "../api/types";
import { SectionReportCard } from "./SectionReportCard";

/**
 * The wait between finishing a section and seeing the Report Card.
 *
 * Grading is a post-section job: one transcription of ~25 minutes of audio plus
 * one judgement call per stimulus, run server-side after she stops. It does not
 * finish inside the request that ends the section, and it must not be tied to
 * this tab staying open.
 *
 * So this polls for the grade rather than waiting on a response. The ABSENCE of
 * a row is the pending signal (ADR 0019): there is no status column to trust,
 * and none she could set herself. If it never arrives, the honest thing to show
 * is that it is still being marked, not an error and certainly not a zero. The
 * sweeper picks up anything this tab missed.
 */

const POLL_MS = 4000;
/** After this, stop asking and tell her it is still being marked. */
const GIVE_UP_MS = 3 * 60 * 1000;
/**
 * How long to wait before asking for the grade a second time.
 *
 * The runner already asked once, fire and forget, and that ask can be lost: the
 * tab closed before it left, the network dropped, the function was cold. One
 * repeat after a decent wait recovers all three.
 *
 * The wait has to be longer than a grading actually takes. At 20 seconds the
 * repeat arrived while the first grading was still transcribing, nothing was on
 * record yet, and both ran to the end: two transcriptions, two sets of verdicts
 * on the same audio, and a card that showed the first while the database kept
 * the second. A real section grades in one to three minutes. The grader now
 * also refuses to start while another grading of the same Attempt is running,
 * so a repeat that does land early costs one request rather than a second
 * grading; this wait is what keeps that from being the ordinary path.
 */
const RETRY_ASK_MS = 120000;

interface SectionReportProps {
  assignmentId: string;
  onDone?: () => void;
}

export function SectionReport({ assignmentId, onDone }: SectionReportProps) {
  const navigate = useNavigate();
  const [grade, setGrade] = useState<SectionGrade | null>(null);
  const [stillWaiting, setStillWaiting] = useState(false);
  const startedAt = useRef(Date.now());

  useEffect(() => {
    let cancelled = false;
    let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout>;
    let askedAgain = false;

    const poll = async () => {
      try {
        const found = await getSectionGrade(assignmentId);
        if (cancelled) return;
        if (found) {
          setGrade(found);
          return;
        }
      } catch {
        // A failed read is not a failed run. Keep waiting; the row either
        // arrives or the give-up copy takes over.
      }
      if (cancelled) return;

      const waited = Date.now() - startedAt.current;
      if (!askedAgain && waited > RETRY_ASK_MS) {
        // Ask once more. Losing the first ask is the likeliest way a finished
        // run ends up with no Report Card, and it is the one failure this
        // screen is in a position to repair by itself.
        askedAgain = true;
        void requestSectionGrading(assignmentId);
      }
      if (waited > GIVE_UP_MS) {
        setStillWaiting(true);
        return;
      }
      timer = setTimeout(poll, POLL_MS);
    };

    void poll();
    return () => {
      cancelled = true;
      clearTimeout(timer);
    };
  }, [assignmentId]);

  const navigate = useNavigate();

   * asking for ever and the only way to find out was to leave and come back,

  const [waitedMs, setWaitedMs] = useState(0);

new='''  /** Whole minutes, for a sentence rather than a counter she watches. */
  const minutesWaited = Math.floor(waitedMs / 60000);

  return (
    <div className="mx-auto max-w-2xl">
      <div className="rounded-[28px] border border-border bg-[hsl(var(--ac-surface))] p-8 text-center shadow-[0_2px_24px_rgba(30,24,16,0.06)] md:p-12">
        <p className="text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.2em] text-muted-foreground">
          Section finished
        </p>
        {/*
          * One live region over both states.
          *
          * The heading and the sentence both change without a page load, and
          * neither was announced: a screen reader user was left on "Marking
          * your run" with no way to know it had become "Still being marked",
          * or that the card had arrived. `aria-live` sits on the wrapper so the
          * pair is read as one update rather than two.
          */}
        <div role="status" aria-live="polite">
          <p className="ac-display mt-3 text-4xl text-foreground">
            {stillWaiting ? "Still being marked" : "Marking your run"}
          </p>
          <p className="mt-4 text-sm text-muted-foreground">
            {stillWaiting
              ? `This one has been marking for ${minutesWaited} minutes, which is longer than the usual one to three. The marking carries on whether or not you stay on this page, and your Report Card will be on this section in the Activity Centre when it lands.`
              : "Your recording is being read back against every detail. That usually takes one to three minutes."}
          </p>
        </div>
        {/*
          * Past the give-up, two real actions.
          *
          * There used to be one button, "Done", and giving up was terminal:
          * the screen stopped asking, said her card would be there "when you
          * come back to it", and gave her nothing to come back with. "Check
          * again" restarts the polling and the ask; the other button says
          * where it goes.
          */}
        <div className="mt-8 flex flex-wrap items-center justify-center gap-3">
          {stillWaiting && (
            <Button
              onClick={() => {
                setStillWaiting(false);
                setRound((n) => n + 1);
              }}
            >
              Check again
            </Button>
          )}
          {onDone && (
            <Button variant={stillWaiting ? "outline" : "outline"} onClick={onDone}>
              Back to the Activity Centre
            </Button>
          )}
        </div>
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}'''

import { render, screen } from "@testing-library/react";
import { MemoryRouter } from "react-router-dom";
import type { SectionGrade } from "../api/types";
import { SectionReport } from "./SectionReport";

 * The screen navigates now.
 *
 * A Report Card can start a fresh Attempt built from this run's weakest
 * stimuli, and that lands the student in the runner, so `SectionReport` calls
 * `useNavigate` and cannot be rendered outside a Router.
 */

  getSectionGrade: (id: string) => getSectionGrade(id),
  requestSectionGrading: (id: string) => requestSectionGrading(id),
  // Reached by the card this screen hands over to. A section nobody has been
  // graded on has none of these, which is also what the database returns.
  listModelRuns: () => Promise.resolve({}),

}));

import { useMemo } from "react";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";
import { bandFor } from "../recall/grading";
import { tallyMisses } from "../recall/misses";
import { SEED_ACTIVITIES } from "../content";
import type { SectionGrade, SectionGradeStub } from "../api/types";

/**
 * Every section run on record, and what keeps slipping across all of them.
 *
 * The drill's own instruction is three a day, six if she can. Nothing in the
 * product knew she had done four: a Report Card is one run, and one run cannot
 * tell her whether she is getting better. Twenty runs can, and they were all
 * already stored.
 *
 * The same component answers both questions worth asking of a pile of runs, and
 * they are different questions:
 *
 * - **Is this working?** Coverage over time, run by run, next to the clock.
 * - **What do I keep dropping?** Not which Details, which KIND of Detail, as a
 *   share of the times that kind came up. "You dropped 'all employees'" is a
 *   fact about a stimulus; "you drop the quantifier two times in three" is a
 *   fact about her, and only the second one changes what she practises.
 *
 * Read-only and derived. Nothing here is stored, so a better grouping is a
 * deploy rather than a backfill, and this cannot disagree with the Report Cards
 * it is built from.
 */

/**
 * A run, in either of the two shapes the app reads them in.
 *
 * The table needs only the summary, which is the light read the section library
 * makes for every run she has ever done. "What keeps slipping" needs the
 * per-stimulus misses, which is the heavier read, so it is made for the recent
 * few and the rest arrive without it. A run with no `stimuli` contributes to the
 * table and not to the tally, rather than being left out of both.
 */
type Run = SectionGradeStub & Partial<Pick<SectionGrade, "stimuli">>;

interface SectionRunHistoryProps {
  grades: Run[];
  /** Whose runs these are, when the reader is not the student. */
  heading?: string;
  emptyMessage?: string;
}

const BAND_TEXT: Record<ReturnType<typeof bandFor>, string> = {
  "at-the-standard": "text-[hsl(var(--ac-emerald-strong))]",
  "nearly-there": "text-[hsl(38_92%_34%)]",
  thin: "text-[hsl(24_85%_42%)]",
  "lost-it": "text-[hsl(350_80%_45%)]",
};

function minutes(seconds: number): string {
  const m = Math.floor(seconds / 60);
  return `${m}:${String(Math.round(seconds % 60)).padStart(2, "0")}`;
}

function attemptDate(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary): string {
  const when = new Date(a.completed_at ?? a.created_at);
  return when.toLocaleDateString(undefined, { day: "numeric", month: "short" });
}

/**
 * A section she was handed and has not opened.
 *
 * Not an Attempt: nothing was recorded, nothing was answered, and calling it
 * "Attempt 1 - not finished" tells her she abandoned a run she never began.
 * `started_at` is the honest test, because a teacher-assigned row and a
 * server-made one both arrive as rows and only one of them has a clock.
 */
function isWaiting(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary): boolean {
  return a.status === "assigned" && !a.started_at;
}

/**
 * What one past Attempt says about itself.
 *
 * Three states, and they are genuinely different: a run that was never
 * finished, a finished one still being marked, and a marked one with a number.
 * The old list said "done" for all three and led nowhere, so an Attempt she had
 * spent twenty-five minutes on was a line of grey text.
 */
function attemptLabel(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary, grade: SectionGradeStub | undefined): string {
  const date = attemptDate(a);
  if (a.status !== "completed") return `${date} · not finished`;
  if (!grade) return `${date} · still being marked`;
  return `${date} · ${grade.section.mean_coverage}% carried`;
}

/**
 * What the row is FOR, said on the row.
 *
 * Celia, on the morning after her first graded section: "I see the percentage,
 * but I'm not seeing a way to look back at the attempt to see what information
 * I was missing in my responses." The Report Card was already at the end of
 * this button and had been for weeks. The row simply never said so. It was
 * 13.5px muted text with a dotted underline, which is what a caption looks
 * like, and the label stopped at the number.
 *
 * A percentage is a verdict she cannot act on. The card behind it is the whole
 * point of the drill, so the row names it.
 */
function attemptAction(
  a: ActivityAssignmentSummary,
  grade: SectionGradeStub | undefined,
): string | null {
  if (a.status !== "completed") return null;
  if (!grade) return "Check back shortly";
  return "See what you missed";
}

description: Say how long a run has been marking
command: cd "C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu" && python - <<'PY'
import io
p='src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionLibrary.tsx'
s=io.open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()

old='''function attemptLabel(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary, grade: SectionGradeStub | undefined): string {
  const date = attemptDate(a);
  if (a.status !== "completed") return `${date} · not finished`;
  if (!grade) return `${date} · still being marked`;
  return `${date} · ${grade.section.mean_coverage}% carried`;
}'''
new='''/**
 * How long a finished run has been waiting for its grade.
 *
 * "Still being marked" is true at four minutes and true at four hours, and she
 * cannot tell those apart, so she either sits waiting on something that is
 * already broken or writes off something that was about to land. A real
 * grading takes one to three minutes; anything much past that is worth her
 * knowing as a number.
 */
function markingMinutes(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary): number | null {
  if (!a.completed_at) return null;
  const ms = Date.now() - new Date(a.completed_at).getTime();
  if (!Number.isFinite(ms) || ms < 0) return null;
  return Math.floor(ms / 60000);
}

/** The clock time a run finished, for a wait long enough that minutes stop helping. */
function finishedAtTime(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary): string | null {
  if (!a.completed_at) return null;
  return new Date(a.completed_at).toLocaleTimeString(undefined, {
    hour: "numeric",
    minute: "2-digit",
  });
}

function attemptLabel(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary, grade: SectionGradeStub | undefined): string {
  const date = attemptDate(a);
  if (a.status !== "completed") return `${date} · not finished`;
  if (!grade) {
    const waited = markingMinutes(a);
    if (waited === null) return `${date} · still being marked`;
    if (waited >= 60) {
      const at = finishedAtTime(a);
      return at ? `${date} · finished ${at}, still being marked` : `${date} · still being marked`;
    }
    if (waited < 1) return `${date} · marking now`;
    return `${date} · marking for ${waited} ${waited === 1 ? "minute" : "minutes"}`;
  }
  return `${date} · ${grade.section.mean_coverage}% carried`;
}'''
assert old in s
s=s.replace(old,new,1)

old2='''  if (a.status !== "completed") return null;
  if (!grade) return "Check back shortly";
  return "See what you missed";'''
new2='''  if (a.status !== "completed") return null;
  if (!grade) {
    /*
     * Past the point where waiting is the right advice, say what to do instead.
     * The row opens the marking screen, which now has a Check again on it, so
     * this is a real instruction rather than a hope.
     */
    const waited = markingMinutes(a);
    return waited !== null && waited >= 10 ? "Open it and check again" : "Check back shortly";

  return "See what you missed";'''

import { Lock } from "lucide-react";
import { useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";
import { Button } from "@/components/ui/button";
import { useUserPermissions } from "@/hooks/useUserPermissions";

/**
 * What a student sees when a route's feature flag is false.
 *
 * It renders as ordinary stage content, not a full-viewport takeover: both
 * shells mount this inside their <Outlet/>, so `min-h-screen` plus a hard zinc
 * background painted a slab over the frame it is meant to sit inside. Tokens
 * only, so it reads in light and dark — the old `text-zinc-300` landed on the
 * outline variant's light `bg-background` and came out at 1.44:1.
 *
 * The old copy ("Premium Content Locked ... contact administration") named no
 * one and led nowhere. A locked door has to say who opens it.
 */
export default function LockedModule() {
  const navigate = useNavigate();
  const permissions = useUserPermissions();

  return (

/** The 26-stimulus section observed in the audit. */
const TWENTY_SIX = pt134;
const CHUNKS = ONE.items[0].chunks;

async function renderRun(overrides: Partial<Parameters<typeof SectionRunner>[0]> = {}) {
  const onComplete = vi.fn();
  const view = render(
    <SectionRunner
      assignmentId="a1"
      studentId="s1"
      items={ONE.items}
      sourceSection={ONE.source_section}
      timeGoalMinutes={ONE.time_goal_minutes}
      onComplete={onComplete}
      // The three-second ready beat is real and tested on its own, below. Every
      // other test here is about the loop AFTER it, so they start where a
      // student starts pressing rather than waiting three seconds each.
      readySeconds={0}
      {...overrides}
    />,
  );
  // The microphone is asked for on mount and answers in a microtask. The ready
  // beat waits for that answer now, so a test that pressed Space before it
  // arrived would be pressing at a held beat rather than at stimulus one.
  await act(async () => {});
  return { ...view, onComplete };
}

const space = () => fireEvent.keyDown(document, { code: "Space" });
const finish = (count: number) => {
  for (let i = 0; i < count * 2; i += 1) space();
};

## assistant

Step 3's code is complete; the full suite is running and I'll commit once it's green. Summary of what changed while I wait for it:

- **The marking wait now has a design past three minutes.** It says how long it has been, both states sit in one `role="status"` region (they were silent to a screen reader before), "Check again" restarts the polling and the ask instead of the screen ending itself, and the other button says where it goes rather than "Done".
- **"Tell Joshua"** appears past the give-up, with the run's id beside it — but only when the student can actually reach Messages, the same rule `LockedModule` follows. I passed that as a prop rather than calling `useUserPermissions()` inside the wait screen, because that hook redirects on a lost session and this is the one screen that must not navigate away on its own.
- **The library row says the age.** "Still being marked" was equally true at four minutes and four hours; it now reads "marking for 4 minutes" or "finished 21:29, still being marked", and past ten minutes it stops saying "Check back shortly" and says "Open it and check again".
- **The upload-failed race is fixed.** `onComplete` fired on `missingRows === 0` alone, so a run whose answers saved and whose audio did not was handed to the marking screen — overwriting its own recovery sentence within a frame, then promising a card that was never coming. The gate is now "did the recording actually land", it no longer asks for a grade there is nothing to grade, and the exit button no longer depends on unsaved *answers*, which left that case with an instruction and no button.

I also ran the sweeper read-only: **nobody is stuck right now** — all 13 finished runs have a complete Report Card.

Two Step 3 items I have not done, deliberately: the server-side inbox message on grade completion, and scheduling the sweeper. Both need an edge-function deploy or cron, which don't happen from a push and are production changes I'd want you to call. `scripts/rrc-ungraded.ts` remains the only thing that finds a refused run, so it still needs running daily until the sweeper is scheduled — I've said that in the script's own header.

/* Elapsed time, in the rail beside the position line.
 *
 * Counting up, never down. The drill is timed and she has to be able to pace a
 * 25-minute section, but a number falling towards zero in the corner of her eye
 * produces exactly the rushed reading this drill treats. The goal sits after it
 * in lighter type so the elapsed figure is the one that reads first.
 */
.ac-section-clock {
  font-family: var(--sec-sans);
  font-size: 13px;
  font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
  color: var(--sec-chrome);
  white-space: nowrap;
}

.ac-section-clock i {
  font-style: normal;
  opacity: 0.55;
}

 * the red is on top of it. */

command: cd "C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu" && git add scripts/rrc-ungraded.ts src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionLibrary.tsx src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionLibrary.test.tsx src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionReport.tsx src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionReport.test.tsx src/features/activity-centre/recall/SectionRunner.tsx src/features/activity-centre/recall/SectionRunner.test.tsx && git commit -F - <<'EOF'
RRC: an ending for the run the grader is slow to mark

A grade that is slow, or one the grader refuses, had no design past the
three minute give-up. The screen said "Your run is saved and your Report
Card will be here when you come back to it", stopped asking, and gave her
nothing to come back with. On 2026-09-08 a student sat on that sentence
for three and a half hours before she messaged.

The wait now says how long it has been, so four minutes and four hours
stop looking identical. Both states sit in one role="status" region;
neither was announced before, so a screen reader user was left on
"Marking your run" with no way to learn it had changed. "Check again"
restarts the polling and the ask, which makes giving up recoverable
rather than terminal, and the other button says where it goes instead of
"Done" on a card she was never shown. Past the give-up she is given the
run id, which is the one thing she could not produce herself.

Said as a sentence rather than a "Tell Joshua" button on purpose. Gating
a button on has_chat_access means mounting useUserPermissions, which
redirects on a lost session, into the one screen whose whole job is to
stay put while a grade lands. Wiring it through the caller moved the same
dependency into ActivityRunner and broke 49 of its tests, which is the
seam saying no.

The section library row says the age too: "marking for 4 minutes", or
"finished 21:29, still being marked", and past ten minutes it stops
saying "Check back shortly" and says "Open it and check again", which is
now a real instruction because that screen has one.

And the upload-failed race. onComplete fired on missingRows === 0 alone,
so a run whose answers saved and whose audio did not was handed to the
marking screen: the recovery sentence telling her the recording was still
on this computer was replaced within a frame by "Marking your run", and
three minutes later by "Still being marked, it will be here when you come
back", which was false. The gate is now whether the recording actually
landed. It no longer asks for a grade there is nothing to grade, and the
exit stops depending on unsaved ANSWERS, which had left the
recording-only failure with an instruction and no button.

Sweeper run read-only while writing this: every finished section older
than ten minutes has a complete Report Card, 13 checked.

Not done here, and both need a production action rather than a push: the
server-side inbox message when a grade lands, and scheduling
sweep-rrc-grading (ADR 0021). Until that is scheduled
scripts/rrc-ungraded.ts is still the only thing that finds a refused run,
and its header now says so.

103 test files, 2748 tests, typecheck clean.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF
git log --oneline -1
[/external_agent_tool_call]

new = """## Step 3: done, 2026-09-09

Commit `3d3d999`, "RRC: an ending for the run the grader is slow to mark". The third P0.

- The marking wait says how long it has been, so four minutes and four hours stop looking identical. Both states sit in one `role="status"` region; neither was announced before.
- "Check again" restarts the polling and the ask, so the give-up is recoverable instead of terminal. The other button says where it goes rather than "Done" on a card she was never shown.
- Past the give-up she gets the run id, the one thing she could not produce herself.
- The section library row says the age: "marking for 4 minutes", or "finished 21:29, still being marked". Past ten minutes it stops saying "Check back shortly" and says "Open it and check again", which is a real instruction now that the screen has one.
- The upload-failed race is fixed. `onComplete` fired on `missingRows === 0` alone, so a run whose answers saved and whose audio did not was handed to the marking screen, overwriting its own recovery sentence within a frame and then promising a card that was never coming. The gate is now whether the recording landed; it no longer asks for a grade there is nothing to grade; and the exit stopped depending on unsaved *answers*, which had left the recording-only failure with an instruction and no button.

**A design decision worth keeping.** "Tell Joshua" is a sentence, not a button. Gating a button on `has_chat_access` means mounting `useUserPermissions`, which redirects on a lost session, into the one screen whose whole job is to stay put while a grade lands. Passing it through the caller moved the same dependency into `ActivityRunner` and broke 49 of its tests. That is the seam saying no, and the sentence does the job.

### Verification

- 103 test files, 2748 tests, all passing. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.
- `scripts/rrc-ungraded.ts` run read-only: every finished section older than ten minutes has a complete Report Card, 13 checked. Nobody is stuck today.
- The marking screen has no browser harness and needs a real ungraded assignment to reach, so it is proved by ten render tests rather than eyeballed. Not fabricating a stuck run on his server.

### Deferred from Step 3, both needing a production action rather than a push

- The server-side inbox message when a grade lands. It is an edge-function change, and edge functions do not deploy from a push.
- Scheduling `sweep-rrc-grading` (ADR 0021). Until it is scheduled, `scripts/rrc-ungraded.ts` is still the only thing that finds a refused run, and its header now says so.

Next: Step 4, runner accessibility."""

/* Read, Recall, Colour, section mode.
 *
 * The v1 runner sits on a dark field because it shows one chunk at a time and
 * nothing during recall: there is barely anything on screen to light. A section
 * is the opposite surface. The whole stimulus is present for the whole
 * stimulus, it is the only thing on screen, and she is reading it closely for
 * twenty minutes. That is a document, and a document belongs on paper.
 *
 * So this screen wears the real section's clothes. `OfficialDrillFrame` already
 * mirrors LawHub -- indigo field, white header rail, one centred white card,
 * Fraunces at 19px -- and that is the surface she will sit on test day. Every
 * value below is taken from it rather than invented, and the answering half is
 * removed rather than restyled. No new dialect: this is the drill room.
 *
 * Everything after the frame exists to keep the text in exactly the same place
 * while its state changes.
 */

.ac-section {
  --sec-field: #1a1635;
  --sec-paper: #ffffff;
  --sec-ink: #111827;
  --sec-rule: #cbd5e1;
  --sec-chrome: #334155;
  --sec-warn: #b45309;
  --sec-sans: "Helvetica Neue", Helvetica, Arial, sans-serif;

  display: flex;
  min-height: 100vh;
  flex-direction: column;
  background: var(--sec-field);
  font-family: var(--sec-sans);
}

/* ---- the white rail ---------------------------------------------------- */

/* One column per child, and the count has to be kept: declared short once, the
   exit wrapped onto a second row and the rail grew the header from 46px to 96px
   on the drill's only screen. Six now -- position, rail, clock, the recording
   mark, Pause, the way out. */
.ac-section-head {
  display: grid;
  grid-template-columns: auto 1fr auto auto auto auto;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 1.25rem;
  padding: 0.75rem 1.5rem;
  border-bottom: 1px solid #e2e8f0;
  background: var(--sec-paper);
  color: var(--sec-chrome);
  font-size: 0.8125rem;
}
[/external_agent_tool_result]

  font-optical-sizing: none;

.ac-section-ready-body {
  max-width: 34ch;
  margin: 0;
  color: #64748b;
  font-size: 0.875rem;
  line-height: 1.65;
}

.ac-section-ready-count {
  color: #94a3b8;
  font-size: 0.8125rem;
  font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
}

/* A break wears the ready beat's clothes on purpose. It is the same kind of
   moment -- the section open, the clock not running, one thing to press -- and
   a second full-card layout for it would be a second thing to keep true. */
=== 604,650 ===
 * the transition is over long before any of them are.
 */
.ac-section.is-finishing {
  /* The Activity Centre's own canvas, by value rather than by eye.
     `--background` is scoped to `.activity-centre`, which this element is not
     inside, and landing two shades off it turns a handover into a visible step
     at the exact moment it is meant to be invisible. Kept beside the token it
     copies so a change to one is a change to look for in the other. */
  background: hsl(38 36% 93%);
  transition: background 900ms cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);
}

.ac-section.is-finishing .ac-section-head {
  opacity: 0;
  transition: opacity 500ms ease;
}

.ac-section.is-finishing .ac-section-paper {
  border-color: transparent;
  box-shadow: 0 1px 3px rgb(0 0 0 / 0.04);
  transition:
    box-shadow 900ms cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1),
    border-color 900ms ease;
}

/* What she reads while it happens. It arrives after the field has started to
   warm, so the sentence appears on paper rather than on the drill room. */
.ac-section-finished {
  margin: 0;
  animation: ac-section-finished-in 700ms cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1) 200ms both;
  color: var(--sec-ink);
  font-family: Fraunces, ui-serif, Georgia, serif;
  font-size: 22px;
  font-optical-sizing: none;
  font-variation-settings: "opsz" 14;
  text-align: center;
}

.ac-section-finished span {
  display: block;
  margin-top: 0.75rem;
  color: #64748b;
  font-family: var(--sec-sans);
  font-size: 0.875rem;
  line-height: 1.6;
}
[/external_agent_tool_result]

import io
