s=io.open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()

# 1. The focus ring. Graphite at 35% is 2.17:1 on paper and 1.00:1 on indigo.
old='''/* The focus ring is graphite everywhere in the drill room, never an accent. */
.ac-section :focus-visible {
  outline: 3px solid rgb(29 29 31 / 0.35);
  outline-offset: 2px;
}'''
new='''/* The focus ring is graphite everywhere in the drill room, never an accent.
 *
 * It used to be graphite at 35 per cent, which computes to 2.17:1 on the paper
 * and the rail and 1.00:1 on the indigo field, where the walkthrough's Hide
 * button lives: an invisible ring on the one control a keyboard user has to
 * find. WCAG 1.4.11 wants 3:1. Solid ink is 16.9:1 on white, and paper is
 * 15.6:1 on the field, so both ends clear it with room. Two pixels rather than
 * three because a solid ring reads heavier than a translucent one at the same
 * width. */
.ac-section :focus-visible {
  outline: 2px solid var(--sec-ink);
  outline-offset: 2px;
}

/* The children that sit on the indigo rather than on paper. */
.ac-section-coach :focus-visible,
.ac-section-ready :focus-visible {
  outline-color: var(--sec-paper);
}'''
assert old in s
s=s.replace(old,new,1)

# 2. The pointer target. PRODUCT.md binds 44x44; both controls are ~30px tall.
old='''.ac-section-pause {
  border: 1px solid var(--sec-rule);'''
new='''.ac-section-pause {
  border: 1px solid var(--sec-chrome);'''
assert old in s
s=s.replace(old,new,1)

anchor='''/* The focus ring is graphite everywhere in the drill room, never an accent.'''
target='''/* Forty-four pixels of pointer target, without forty-four pixels of rail.
 *
 * Pause and Leave measure 62x30 and 59x29 on a desktop and 54x30 and 51x29 on
 * a phone, where the header rule takes their padding down again. PRODUCT.md
 * binds 44x44 by being that size, and both are wide enough already; only the
 * height is short.
 *
 * Given as an overlay rather than a `min-height`, because the rail is a grid
 * row and growing the controls grows the header: it went from 46px to 96px once
 * before, on the drill's only screen, and every other rule in this file is
 * fighting for the reading area on a 13-inch laptop. The overlay is centred on
 * the control, so it costs no layout and no pixels of paper. */
.ac-section-pause,
.ac-section-exit {
  position: relative;
}

.ac-section-pause::after,
.ac-section-exit::after {
  content: "";
  position: absolute;
  left: 0;
  right: 0;
  top: 50%;
  height: 44px;
  transform: translateY(-50%);
}

''' + anchor
assert anchor in s
s=s.replace(anchor,target,1)

# 3. The inks the audit measured under AA.
old='''.ac-section-ready-count {
  color: #94a3b8;'''

assert old in s
s=s.replace(old,new,1)

old='''.ac-section-finished span {

  margin-top: 0.75rem;

  // One key, and only Space. Bound on the document because the loop must not
  // depend on anything holding focus: a stray click on the page would otherwise
  // silently stop her being able to advance.
  useEffect(() => {
    if (done) return;
    const onKey = (e: KeyboardEvent) => {
      if (e.code !== "Space") return;
      /*
       * Anything that takes typing keeps its own spaces.
       *
       * This is bound on the document and it calls `preventDefault`, so every
       * element the list forgets is an element the space bar stops working in
       * while a section is open. It listed BUTTON and INPUT and not TEXTAREA or
       * a contenteditable, which are exactly what a message box is made of --
       * and a student typing into one gets her words back with every space
       * removed, with nothing on screen to explain it.
       */
      const target = e.target as HTMLElement | null;
      if (
        target &&
        (target.tagName === "BUTTON" ||
          target.tagName === "INPUT" ||
          target.tagName === "TEXTAREA" ||
          target.tagName === "SELECT" ||
          target.isContentEditable)
      )
        return;

  /**
   * Step away, and come back to the same run.
   *
   * The recording is paused FIRST and the clock only stops if it actually did.
   * A recorder that kept writing while the timeline stopped would put the whole
   * break inside the file with nothing in the index to say it is there, and
   * every segment after it would point minutes away from the words it names --
   * a wrong grade that looks like a right one, which is the failure this whole
   * feature has to not introduce. If the browser will not pause, she is told so
   * and the section carries on running rather than half-pausing.
   */
  const pauseRun = useCallback(() => {
    if (done || paused || countdown !== null || saving.current) return;
    if (!pauseRecording()) {
      setPauseError(
        "This browser will not pause the recording, so the section cannot pause. Keep going, or leave and start again in Chrome.",
      );
      return;

    pausedSince.current = now();

   * Escape did nothing, and three Tabs put focus on the Pause button behind it,

  useEffect(() => {

import { beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";

import pt105 from "../content/read-recall-colour-pt105-s2.json";
import { SectionRunner } from "./SectionRunner";
import { discard, listPendingRuns } from "./recordingBackup";

/**
 * Leaving a section, and what must die with it.
 *
 * On 2026-09-07 a student opened a section at 23:28, left it, and came back at
 * 00:06 to run it properly. The first sitting's recorder was never stopped:
 * nothing stopped it on unmount, so it kept the microphone open and kept
 * writing its backup pieces for the next two hours, through her real run and
 * past the end of it. When she reloaded the page after finishing, that orphan
 * finally went quiet, the recovery pass took it for a crashed run, and
 * uploaded its 116-minute file OVER the 66-minute recording her real sitting
 * had just stored. The grader then refused the run: every window sat 37
 * minutes before its speech.
 *
 * Two rules, asserted here because both failures are silent. A recorder does
 * not outlive the component that started it, and a run she walked out of has
 * no backup worth recovering.
 */

type Row = Record<string, unknown>;
const attemptRows = { data: [] as Row[], error: null as unknown };
vi.mock("@/integrations/supabase/client", () => ({
  supabase: {
    from: () => ({
      upsert: () => Promise.resolve({ error: null }),
      update: () => ({ eq: () => Promise.resolve({ error: null }) }),
      select: () => ({
        eq: () => ({
          eq: () => ({ order: () => ({ limit: () => Promise.resolve(attemptRows) }) }),
        }),

    storage: { from: () => ({ upload: () => Promise.resolve({ error: null }) }) },

command: cd "C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu" && cat > src/features/activity-centre/recall/SectionStage.a11y.test.tsx <<'EOF'
import { fireEvent, render, screen } from "@testing-library/react";
import { describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import { SectionStage } from "./SectionStage";
import type { SectionStimulus } from "./section";

/*
 * What the drill room owed a keyboard, and did not pay.
 *
 * The confirm carried `aria-modal="true"` and none of what that attribute
 * promises: Escape did nothing, and three Tabs put focus on the Pause button
 * behind it, where Space still advanced the drill underneath a dialog asking
 * whether to throw twenty-five minutes away. The screen also had no heading of
 * any kind, so a screen reader's heading list was empty on the one page a
 * student spends half an hour inside.
 */

const stimulus: SectionStimulus = {
  slug: "pt-105-s2-q01",
  chunks: [{ n: 1, text: "The council's own survey contradicts the figure it published." }],
} as SectionStimulus;

function renderStage(over: Partial<Parameters<typeof SectionStage>[0]> = {}) {
  return render(
    <SectionStage
      stimulus={stimulus}
      phase="working"
      stimulusNumber={7}
      totalStimuli={26}
      error={null}
      elapsedSeconds={461}
      sectionName="PT105.S2"
      goalSeconds={25 * 60}
      {...over}
    />,
  );
}

describe("the drill room's heading", () => {
  it("names the drill and the section, for a heading list that was empty", () => {
    renderStage();
    const h1 = screen.getByRole("heading", { level: 1 });
    expect(h1.textContent).toBe("Read, Recall, Colour, PT105.S2");
  });

  it("still says something when the section has no name", () => {
    renderStage({ sectionName: undefined });
    expect(screen.getByRole("heading", { level: 1 }).textContent).toBe("Read, Recall, Colour");
  });
});

describe("the clock", () => {
  it("shows the goal it is counting against", () => {
    const { container } = renderStage();
    // The elapsed figure reads first; the goal sits after it in lighter type.
    // `.ac-section-clock i` was styled for this when the clock shipped and
    // nothing ever rendered it, so she paced against a target she could not see.
    const clock = container.querySelector(".ac-section-clock");
    expect(clock?.textContent).toBe("7:41 of 25:00");
  });

  it("draws no goal when there is none to draw", () => {
    const { container } = renderStage({ goalSeconds: undefined });
    expect(container.querySelector(".ac-section-clock")?.textContent).toBe("7:41");
    expect(container.querySelector(".ac-section-clock i")).toBeNull();
  });
});

describe("the leave confirmation", () => {
  const confirming = (onCancelExit: () => void) =>
    renderStage({
      onExit: vi.fn(),
      confirmingExit: true,
      onConfirmExit: vi.fn(),
      onCancelExit,
    });

  it("closes on Escape, and Escape means keep going", () => {
    const onCancelExit = vi.fn();
    const onConfirmExit = vi.fn();
    renderStage({
      onExit: vi.fn(),
      confirmingExit: true,
      onConfirmExit,
      onCancelExit,
    });
    fireEvent.keyDown(document, { key: "Escape" });
    expect(onCancelExit).toHaveBeenCalled();
    // A reflex keypress must never be the thing that throws the run away.
    expect(onConfirmExit).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
  });

  it("keeps Tab inside itself", () => {
    const { container } = confirming(vi.fn());
    const dialog = container.querySelector(".ac-section-confirm") as HTMLElement;
    const stops = [...dialog.querySelectorAll("button")];
    expect(stops).toHaveLength(2);

    stops[stops.length - 1].focus();
    fireEvent.keyDown(document, { key: "Tab" });
    expect(document.activeElement).toBe(stops[0]);

    fireEvent.keyDown(document, { key: "Tab", shiftKey: true });
    expect(document.activeElement).toBe(stops[stops.length - 1]);
  });

  it("pulls focus back when it has escaped the dialog", () => {
    const { container } = confirming(vi.fn());
    const dialog = container.querySelector(".ac-section-confirm") as HTMLElement;
    const outside = container.querySelector(".ac-section-exit") as HTMLElement;
    outside.focus();
    fireEvent.keyDown(document, { key: "Tab" });
    expect(dialog.contains(document.activeElement)).toBe(true);
  });

  it("takes the rest of the screen out of the tree while it is up", () => {
    const { container } = confirming(vi.fn());
    expect(container.querySelector("header")?.hasAttribute("inert")).toBe(true);
    expect(container.querySelector("main")?.hasAttribute("inert")).toBe(true);
  });

  it("leaves the screen alone when it is not up", () => {
    const { container } = renderStage({ onExit: vi.fn() });
    expect(container.querySelector("header")?.hasAttribute("inert")).toBe(false);
    expect(container.querySelector("main")?.hasAttribute("inert")).toBe(false);
  });
});
EOF

    await renderRun({ onExit, readySeconds: 3 });
    fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole("button", { name: /leave/i }));
    expect(onExit).toHaveBeenCalled();
  });
});

Step 4's code is done and measured live. Waiting on the suite before I commit.

What's in it, all verified in the browser rather than asserted:

- **The focus ring was invisible.** Graphite at 35% computes to 2.17:1 on the paper and **1.00:1 on the indigo field**, where the walkthrough's Hide button sits — a focus ring a keyboard user literally cannot see. It's now solid ink, measured at **17.74:1** on paper, with a paper-coloured ring on the field at **17.35:1**. WCAG 1.4.11 wants 3:1.
- **44px pointer targets without a fatter rail.** Pause and Leave measure 62×30 and 59×29. Both are wide enough already; only height was short. I gave them a centred 44px overlay rather than `min-height`, because the header is a grid row and growing the controls grows the rail — it went 46px→96px once before, and every other rule in that file is fighting for reading area on a 13" laptop. Measured live: hit area 44px, visual size and rail height unchanged.
- **The leave dialog now does what `aria-modal` claimed.** Escape closes it (and Escape means *keep going* — a reflex keypress must never throw the run away), Tab is trapped, and the rest of the screen goes `inert`. I also found the other half: the Space handler had no guard, so with focus behind the dialog the drill kept advancing underneath a question about throwing it away. Guarded, with a test.
- **The goal is finally on screen.** `.ac-section-clock i` had been styled for it since the clock shipped and nothing ever rendered it, so she paced against a target she couldn't see. Now reads "12:34 of 25:00", against *her* goal so an accommodation isn't paced against someone else's.
- **A heading, where there were none.** The drill's only screen had an empty heading list; there's now a visually hidden `h1` naming the drill and section.
- Ready-count ink was #94a3b8 at 2.56:1; the handover sentence sat at 4.17:1 on the warmed ground. Both now `--sec-chrome`, 10.35:1.
- The `space` hint no longer renders on a touch device, where it named a key she has no way to press.

I also gave the Activity Lab the section name and goal — without them, the heading and the goal were the two things on that surface nobody could look at.

One honest limit: scrolled screenshots come back blank from this browser pane, same as the earlier session, so the runner checks are DOM-measured and computed, not eyeballed.

RRC: what the drill room owed a keyboard

The focus ring was graphite at 35 per cent: 2.17:1 on the paper and
1.00:1 on the indigo field, where the walkthrough's Hide button sits. A
focus ring a keyboard user cannot see is not a focus ring. Solid ink
measures 17.74:1 on the paper and paper measures 17.35:1 on the field,
both taken in the browser rather than reasoned about.

The confirm carried aria-modal="true" and none of what the attribute
promises. Escape did nothing. Three Tabs put focus on Pause behind it,
and Space still ran the loop from there, so she could be asked whether to
throw twenty-five minutes away while the section carried on advancing
underneath the question. Escape now closes it and means keep going, Tab
is trapped, the rest of the screen goes inert, and the Space handler
refuses while the question is up. That last one is the half a focus trap
alone would not have fixed.

Pause and Leave are 62x30 and 59x29 against PRODUCT.md's 44x44. Both are
wide enough; only the height was short, and it is given as a centred
overlay rather than a min-height because the rail is a grid row: growing
the controls grows the header, which went from 46px to 96px once before,
and every other rule in that file is fighting for reading area on a
13-inch laptop. The hit box measures 44px with the rail unchanged.

The goal is on screen for the first time. `.ac-section-clock i` has been
styled for it since the clock shipped and nothing ever rendered it, so
she paced a 25-minute section against a target she could not see. It
reads "12:34 of 25:00", against HER goal, because an accommodation scales
it and pacing her against someone else's is worse than showing none.

Also: a visually hidden h1, on a screen whose heading list was empty and
which a student sits inside for half an hour; the ready countdown off
#94a3b8 (2.56:1) and the handover sentence off #64748b (4.17:1 on the
warmed ground it actually lands on); the Pause border off --sec-rule
(1.48:1, and it is the control's only boundary); and no "space" hint on a
device with no Space key, where it named a key she cannot press beside
the button she can.

The Activity Lab gets the section name and the goal, without which the
heading and the goal were the two things on this surface nobody could
look at.

Scrolled screenshots come back blank from the browser pane, so these are
DOM-measured and computed in the page rather than eyeballed.

104 test files, 2759 tests, typecheck clean.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

[external_agent_tool_result]
export const FOCUS_MIN = 5;
export const FOCUS_MAX = 10;

export interface FocusSet {
  slugs: string[];
  stimuli: SectionStimulusGrade[];
  /** How many of them were actually weak, as against padding. */
  weakCount: number;
}

/** Did this stimulus fall short on any criterion the method scores? */
export function isWeak(s: SectionStimulusGrade): boolean {
  if (s.reciting) return true;
  if (s.band !== "at-the-standard") return true;
  if (s.coloured === false) return true;
  if (s.readGiven === false) return true;
  if (s.readMatches === false) return true;
  return false;
}

/** Worst first: a recital, then the lowest coverage, then authored order. */
function severity(s: SectionStimulusGrade): number {
  return (s.reciting ? -1000 : 0) + (s.percent ?? 0);
}

/**
 * Which stimuli to run again, or null when there is nothing to fix.
 *
 * Never throws and never invents a stimulus: everything returned came from the
 * grade it was given, so the slugs are always a subset of the run's own.
 */
export function pickFocusSet(
  grade: Pick<SectionGrade, "stimuli">,
  opts: { min?: number; max?: number } = {},
): FocusSet | null {
  const min = opts.min ?? FOCUS_MIN;
  const max = opts.max ?? FOCUS_MAX;
  const all = (grade.stimuli ?? []).filter((s) => typeof s?.slug === "string" && s.slug !== "");
  if (all.length === 0) return null;

  const ordered = [...all].sort((a, b) => severity(a) - severity(b));
  const weak = ordered.filter(isWeak);
  if (weak.length === 0) return null;

  const chosen: SectionStimulusGrade[] = [];
  for (const s of ordered) {
    if (chosen.length >= max) break;
    if (isWeak(s) || chosen.length < min) chosen.push(s);
  }

  // Back into the order she ran them in, so the redo reads like a section.
  const inOrder = chosen.sort(
    (a, b) => (a.item_order ?? all.indexOf(a)) - (b.item_order ?? all.indexOf(b)),
  );

  return {
    slugs: inOrder.map((s) => s.slug),
    stimuli: inOrder,
    weakCount: Math.min(weak.length, inOrder.length),
  };
}
[/external_agent_tool_result]

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Darken the band inks and add the band word
command: cd "C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu" && python - <<'PY'
import io
p='src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionRunHistory.tsx'
s=io.open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()

old='''const BAND_TEXT: Record<ReturnType<typeof bandFor>, string> = {
  "at-the-standard": "text-[hsl(var(--ac-emerald-strong))]",
  "nearly-there": "text-[hsl(38_92%_34%)]",
  thin: "text-[hsl(24_85%_42%)]",
  "lost-it": "text-[hsl(350_80%_45%)]",
};'''
new='''/**
 * The band, in ink and in a word.
 *
 * The old inks measured 3.79:1 and 3.85:1 on the lab's ground and 4.24:1 and
 * 4.31:1 on the Workroom page, all under AA for body text, and the band was
 * carried by colour alone: a reader who cannot separate amber from red learned
 * nothing from this column, and neither did anyone reading it in grayscale.
 * These are darkened to clear 4.5:1 on both grounds, and every row now says
 * which band it is as well as showing it.
 */
const BAND_TEXT: Record<ReturnType<typeof bandFor>, string> = {
  "at-the-standard": "text-[hsl(var(--ac-emerald-strong))]",
  "nearly-there": "text-[hsl(38_92%_27%)]",
  thin: "text-[hsl(24_85%_34%)]",
  "lost-it": "text-[hsl(350_80%_38%)]",
};

/** The word beside the number, so the colour is never the only carrier. */
const BAND_WORD: Record<ReturnType<typeof bandFor>, string> = {
  "at-the-standard": "at the standard",
  "nearly-there": "nearly there",
  thin: "thin",
  "lost-it": "worth another read",
};'''
assert old in s
s=s.replace(old,new,1)

old='''                  <td className={cn("py-2 pr-4 tabular-nums", BAND_TEXT[band])}>
                    {g.section.mean_coverage}%
                  </td>'''
new='''                  <td className={cn("py-2 pr-4 tabular-nums", BAND_TEXT[band])}>
                    {g.section.mean_coverage}%
                    {/* Said, not only coloured. Visually hidden because the
                        column is a scan of numbers and a word per row would
                        drown it; the colour stays as the fast read for anyone
                        who can use it. */}
                    <span className="sr-only">, {BAND_WORD[band]}</span>
                  </td>'''
assert old in s
io.open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8',newline='').write(s.replace(old,new,1))
print('ok')
PY

description: Add the roving tabindex toolbar
command: cd "C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu" && python - <<'PY'
import io
p='src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionReportCard.tsx'
s=io.open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()

old='''          <div className="rc__ledger mt-3.5">
            {stimuli.map((st, i) => {
              const m = st.ungraded ? "ungraded" : st.reciting ? "recited" : BAND[st.band].mark;
              return (
                <button
                  key={st.slug}
                  type="button"
                  className="rc__tick"
                  aria-current={i === openStimulus}
                  onClick={() => setOpenStimulus(i)}
                >'''
new='''          {/*
            * One tab stop, and the arrows do the rest.
            *
            * Twenty-two ticks were twenty-two tab stops, so reaching anything
            * below the ledger by keyboard meant pressing Tab twenty-two times
            * through a row of marks. A toolbar with a roving tabindex is the
            * pattern for a group like this: Tab lands on whichever stimulus is
            * open, the arrows move between them, Home and End go to the ends.
            */}
          <div
            className="rc__ledger mt-3.5"
            role="toolbar"
            aria-label="The run, stimulus by stimulus"
            aria-orientation="horizontal"
            onKeyDown={(e) => {
              const last = stimuli.length - 1;
              const to =
                e.key === "ArrowRight" || e.key === "ArrowDown"
                  ? Math.min(openStimulus + 1, last)
                  : e.key === "ArrowLeft" || e.key === "ArrowUp"
                    ? Math.max(openStimulus - 1, 0)
                    : e.key === "Home"
                      ? 0
                      : e.key === "End"
                        ? last
                        : null;
              if (to === null) return;
              e.preventDefault();
              setOpenStimulus(to);
              // Focus follows selection, which is what makes the arrows read as
              // moving through the ledger rather than scrubbing something else.
              tickRefs.current[to]?.focus();
            }}
          >
            {stimuli.map((st, i) => {
              const m = st.ungraded ? "ungraded" : st.reciting ? "recited" : BAND[st.band].mark;
              return (
                <button
                  key={st.slug}
                  type="button"
                  className="rc__tick"
                  aria-current={i === openStimulus}
                  tabIndex={i === openStimulus ? 0 : -1}
                  ref={(el) => {
                    tickRefs.current[i] = el;
                  }}
                  onClick={() => setOpenStimulus(i)}
                >'''
assert old in s
s=s.replace(old,new,1)
io.open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8',newline='').write(s)
print('ok')

# A tick is a button whose accessible name now carries its band and state, so

helper='''/**
 * One tick in the ledger, by its position.
 *
 * The accessible name carries the band and the open state as well as the
 * number, because a ledger of twenty-two identical "Stimulus N" buttons told a
 * screen reader nothing about the shape of the run. Tests reach a tick by role
 * and leading number so the rest of that name is free to improve.

'''

    );

    expect(levels[0]).toBe(1);

      expect(levels[i], `heading ${i} skips a level`).toBeLessThanOrEqual(levels[i - 1] + 1);
    }
  });

  it("names itself and its band in one heading", () => {
    render(<SectionReportCard grade={grade} loadModelRuns={noModelRuns} />);
    const h1 = screen.getByRole("heading", { level: 1 });

    expect(h1.textContent).toContain("At the standard");
  });

  it("carries no side stripe, which DESIGN.md bans", () => {
    const { container } = render(<SectionReportCard grade={grade} loadModelRuns={noModelRuns} />);
    // It was `border-l-2` in `--rc-signal`, the colour the brief reserves for
    // sound, on the one sentence that asks her to do something.
    expect(container.querySelector('[class*="border-l-2"]')).toBeNull();
  });

describe("the ledger as one control", () => {

    const ticks = [...document.querySelectorAll(".rc__tick")];
    const reachable = ticks.filter((t) => t.getAttribute("tabindex") === "0");
    expect(ticks.length).toBeGreaterThan(1);
    // Tab lands on whichever stimulus is open; the arrows do the rest.
    expect(reachable).toHaveLength(1);
    expect(reachable[0].getAttribute("aria-current")).toBe("true");
  });

    fireEvent.keyDown(ledger, { key: "ArrowRight" });

    expect(document.activeElement).toBe(tick(2));

    fireEvent.keyDown(ledger, { key: "End" });

    fireEvent.keyDown(ledger, { key: "Home" });

    render(<SectionReportCard grade={grade} loadModelRuns={noModelRuns} />);

RRC: the Report Card says what it means, and can be read by keyboard

The focus set named the wrong stimulus. `item_order` is the authored
position and 1-based, which is how the ledger, the open stimulus and its
heading all read it; the chips read it as `(item_order ?? i) + 1`, so
every chip named the stimulus after the one it meant. She was sent to run
stimulus 15 again over a fault in 14, with the ledger beside it
disagreeing. Nothing tested the focus set at all, which is how it
shipped. The new test is mutation-checked: put the bug back and it fails
with "Stimulus 12" where 11 was meant.

The card had no heading of any kind. Eight sections, a ledger of
twenty-two, and a screen reader's heading list that was empty. The two
lines that were already the title become one h1 rather than a third being
invented, so it reads "Report Card, Attempt 2, At the standard" and the
pixels do not move; the open stimulus is the h2 its movements hang from,
and the movements are h3.

The ledger was twenty-two tab stops, so reaching anything below it by
keyboard meant twenty-two presses through a row of marks. It is a toolbar
with a roving tabindex now: Tab lands on the open stimulus, the arrows
move, Home and End go to the ends, and focus follows the selection. Its
ticks also said only "Stimulus N", so the band and the recital -- the
whole content of a tick -- were carried by colour and shape alone.

The run history's band inks were 3.79:1 and 3.85:1 on the lab ground and
4.24 and 4.31 on the Workroom page, and the band was colour-only.
Darkened and measured in the running app: 5.53/6.34, 5.35/6.13,
6.26/7.17, with the band said in a word beside the number.

Also: the border-l-2 side stripe is gone, which DESIGN.md bans and which
was drawn in --rc-signal, the colour the brief reserves for sound; the
transport pills go 34px to 44px; "Hear it" and the disclosure summaries
get the runner's centred-overlay target, because both sit inline and
cannot grow their boxes without moving the prose around them.

Not done, and not claimed: the always-mounted status region, mapping
recall.verdict to words, "Attempt" for "run", the 72ch escape, and
flattening the four nested bordered boxes.

The ledger ticks stay under 44px WIDE and that is deliberate. Twenty-two
to twenty-six of them at 44px is 968 to 1144px inside an 820px card. They
are 44 tall and clear WCAG 2.5.8's 24x24 with their gaps; meeting
PRODUCT.md's stricter 44x44 would mean the ledger stops being a
single-glance picture of the run, which is the whole point of it. That is
a product call rather than a CSS one.

104 test files, 2767 tests, typecheck clean.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF

# Session Handoff — RRC drill fix plan, Steps 1–5 built, committed and deployed

## Where it started
Picked up from a prior handoff whose deliverable was `RRC-DRILL-AUDIT-CRITIQUE-2026-09-09.md` (audit + critique + 12-step fix plan, no code changed). This session's job was to get Joshua's answers to the three gating decisions, then execute the plan. He answered, then drove it step by step: "keep going with step 5", "commit and push it", "do it" (deploy). Constraint that emerged mid-session: another agent is committing to the same repo and working tree concurrently.

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- **Pause stays; the copy tells the truth.** Six student-facing statements denied a working Pause button. One sentence now everywhere: "You can pause: the text hides and the clock stops while you are away, and you cannot go back to a stimulus." Commit `1cd268f` lineage; guard at `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\src\features\activity-centre\pause-copy.test.ts`.
- **Percentages allowed, per stimulus and section-level.** Joshua's answer. Affects Step 7, not yet built.
- **The runner's LawHub mirror is sanctioned in the records.** Joshua's answer. Step 11, not yet built.
- **Step 2 — one method, one source.** Four step labels + new `short` one-line definitions authored only in `read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json`, read via `src\features\activity-centre\recall\method.ts`. Walkthrough no longer coaches the two faults the card marks. Commit `1cd268f`.
- **Correction to the audit:** the card's "Your Read" was NOT a violation. ADR 0025 (2026-09-03) postdates ADR 0012 and reinstated The Read as the second graded number. `CONTEXT.md` was stale and caused the misdiagnosis; now defines **The Read** and **the move**. Also restored `coach.rubric[6]` from "Score The Recall" to "Score The Read" — not a live grading bug, `grade-rrc-section` builds its own prompt.
- **Step 3 — slow-grade ending.** Elapsed minutes, one `role="status"`, "Check again" restarts polling, "Back to the Activity Centre", run id past give-up. Library row says marking age. Upload-failed race fixed: `onComplete` now gated on the recording actually landing. Commit `3d3d999`.
- **"Tell Joshua" is a sentence, not a button** — gating it needs `useUserPermissions`, which redirects on lost session; wiring via the caller broke 49 `ActivityRunner` tests. Seam said no.
- **Step 4 — runner accessibility.** Focus ring 2.17:1/1.00:1 → measured 17.74:1 / 17.35:1. Leave dialog: Escape, focus trap, `inert`, plus a Space guard in `SectionRunner` (drill advanced behind the dialog). 44px targets via centred overlay (rail height preserved). Goal beside clock ("12:34 of 25:00") — dead CSS since the clock shipped. Visually hidden `h1`. Commit `8120fdf`.
- **Step 5 — Report Card.** Focus-set off-by-one fixed and **mutation-tested**. Full h1/h2/h3 outline (card had none). Ledger → toolbar with roving tabindex. Tick names carry band + state. Band inks darkened, measured 5.53/6.34, 5.35/6.13, 6.26/7.17. `border-l-2` stripe removed. Commit `b1a3f37`.
- **Deployed to production**, verified by grepping 375 live JS chunks + all CSS off lsatprep.study.

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: **Steps 6–12** of the fix plan. Step 6 (door a11y + perf: 191 animations at rest, `aria-live` firing every 1.5–4.2s, H1→H3→H2 outline) is next in sequence.

- Deferred: vault `wrapup` — `wiki/tasks.md`, `hot.md`, `log.md` not updated this session.
- OPEN: **Should the ledger ticks meet PRODUCT.md's 44×44?** They are 44 tall but 28–30 wide; 22–26 at 44px is 968–1144px inside an 820px card. They clear WCAG 2.5.8's 24×24. Meeting 44×44 means the ledger stops being a single-glance picture of the run. Product call, raised, not answered.
- OPEN: **Do the two concurrent agents get separate branches?** Both sessions commit to `main` in the same working tree and both are authored "Joshua Fisseha".
